<div class='question question'>

Eleven pencils cost $\pounds 4.95$. How much would seven pencils cost?

- $\pounds 3.50$
- $\pounds 3.20$
- $\pounds 4.00$
- $\pounds 3.15$
- $\pounds 4.15$ 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 1 pencil}                       &= \dfrac {4.95} {11} \\\\
\text{Cost of 7 pencils}                      &= 7 \times \dfrac {4.95} {11} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {7 \times 4.95} {11} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {7 \times 495} {11 \times 100} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {7 \times 11 \times 45} {11 \times 100} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {7 \times \cancel {11} \times 45} {\cancel {11} \times 100} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {315} {100} \\\\
                                              &= \pounds 3.15
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Let the cost of seven pencils $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
11 \ \text{pencils} : \pounds 4.95              &= 7 \ \text{pencils} : \pounds a  \\\\
\dfrac {11} {4.95}                              &= \dfrac {7} {a} \\\\
11 \times a                                     &= 7 \times 4.95 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {7 \times 4.95} {11} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {7 \times 495} {11 \times 100} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {7 \times 11 \times 45} {11 \times 100} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {7 \times \cancel {11} \times 45} {\cancel {11} \times 100} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {315} {100} \\\\
a                                               &= \pounds 3.15
\end{aligned}
$

Cost of $7$ pencils $= \pounds 3.15$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{pencils}                &:& \text{cost} \\\\
= & 11                            &:& \pounds 4.95 \\\\
= & 1                             &:& \dfrac{4.95} {11} \\\\
= & 1 \times 7                    &:& \dfrac{4.95 \times 7} {11} \\\\
= & 7                             &:& \dfrac{495 \times 7} {11 \times 100} \\\\
= & 7                             &:& \dfrac{11 \times 45 \times 7} {11 \times 100} \\\\
= & 7                             &:& \dfrac{\cancel {11} \times 45 \times 7} {\cancel {11} \times 100} \\\\
= & 7                             &:& \dfrac{315} {100} \\\\
= & 7                             &:& \pounds 3.15
\end{array}
$

Cost of $7$ pencils $= \pounds 3.15$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>D</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 3.15$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<div class='question question'>

Pens cost $35 \ \text{p}$ each. How much money will a box of $25$ pens cost?

- $\pounds 8.40$
- $\pounds 8.75$
- $\pounds 87.50$
- $\pounds 865.00$
- $\pounds 9.25$    

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of } 25 \text{ pens}   &= 25 \times 35 \\\\
                                  &= 875  \ \text{p} \\\\
                                  &= \pounds 8.75
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>B</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 8.75$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of } 25 \text{ pens}   &= 25 \times 35 \\\\
                                  &= 875  \ \text{p} \\\\
                                  &= \pounds 8.75
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>B</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 8.75$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Rob works $40 \ \text{hours}$ a week and earns $\pounds 440$. What is his hourly rate of pay?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Rob's hourly pay}    &= \dfrac {440} {40} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {40 \times 11} {40} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {\cancel {40} \times 11} {\cancel {40}} \\\\
                            &= \pounds 11
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 11$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Rob's hourly pay}    &= \dfrac {440} {40} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {40 \times 11} {40} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {\cancel {40} \times 11} {\cancel {40}} \\\\
                            &= \pounds 11
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 11$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

If $750 \ \text{grams}$ of pasta cost $90 \ \text{p}$ find the cost of $1 \ \text{kilogram}$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 1 gram pasta}         &= \dfrac {90} {750} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {9 \cancel 0} {75 \cancel 0} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {9} {75} \ \text{p} \\\\
\text{Cost of 1000 gram pasta}      &= \dfrac {9} {75} \times 1000 \\\\
\text{Cost of 1 kilogram pasta}     &= \dfrac {9 \times 1000} {75} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3 \times 3 \times 25 \times 40} {25 \times 3} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel 3 \times \cancel {25} \times 40} {\cancel {25} \times \cancel 3} \\\\
                                    &= 3 \times 40 \\\\
                                    &= 120 \ \text{p} \\\\
                                    &= \pounds 1.20
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Let price of pasta $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
750 \ \text{grams} : 90 \ \text{pence}          &= 1000 \ \text{grams} : a \ \text{pence} \\\\
\dfrac{750} {90}                                &= \dfrac {1000} {a} \\\\
750 \times a                                    &= 90 \times 1000 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {90 \times 1000} {750} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3 \times 30 \times 250 \times 4} {3 \times 250} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 30 \times \cancel {250} \times 4} {\cancel 3 \times \cancel {250}} \\\\
a                                               &= 30 \times 4 \\\\
a                                               &= 120 \ \text{pence} \\\\
a                                               &= \pounds 1.20
\end{aligned}
$

Price of $1 \ \text{kilogram}$ of pasta $= 120 \ \text{p}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{pasta}                                  &:& \text{cost} \\\\
= & 750 \ \text{g}                                &:& 90 \ \text{p} \\\\
= & 1                                             &:& \dfrac {90} {750} \\\\
= & 1 \times 1000 \ \text{g}                      &:& \dfrac {90 \times 1000} {750} \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{kg}                                 &:& \dfrac {3 \times 30 \times 250 \times 4} {3 \times 250} \\\\
= & 1                                             &:& \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 30 \times \cancel {250} \times 4} {\cancel 3 \times \cancel {250}} \\\\
= & 1                                             &:& 30 \times 4 \\\\
= & 1                                             &:& 120 \ \text{p} \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{kg}                                 &:& \pounds 1.20
\end{array}
$

Price of $1 \ \text{kilogram}$ of pasta $= \pounds 1.20$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 1.20$

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingcodeblock'>

following:

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The number of centimetres in $4 \ \text{inches}$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of centimetres $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
12 \ \text{inches} : 30 \ \text{cm}             &= 4 \ \text{inches} : a \ \text{cm} \\\\
\dfrac {12} {30}                                &= \dfrac {4} {a} \\\\
12a                                             &= 4 \times 30 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {4 \times 30} {12} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {4 \times 3 \times 10} {4 \times 3} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 3 \times 10} {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 3} \\\\
a                                               &= 10 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Number of centimetres in $4 \ \text{inches} = 10 \ \text{cm}$ 

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{inches}                       &:& \text{centimeters} \\\\
= & 12                                  &:& 30 \\\\
= & 1                                   &:& \dfrac {30} {12} \\\\
= & 1 \times 4                          &:& \dfrac {30 \times 4} {12} \\\\
= & 4                                   &:& \dfrac {3 \times 10 \times 4} {3 \times 4} \\\\
= & 4                                   &:& \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 10 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 3 \times \cancel 4} \\\\
= & 4 \ \text{inches}                   &:& 10 \ \text{cm}
\end{array}
$

Number of centimetres in $4 \ \text{inches} = 10 \ \text{cm}$ 

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$10 \ \text{cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The number of inches in $5 \ \text{centimetres}$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of inches $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
30 \ \text{cm} : 12 \ \text{inches}             &= 5 \ \text{cm} : a \ \text{inches} \\\\
\dfrac {30} {12}                                &= \dfrac {5} {a} \\\\
30a                                             &= 5 \times 12 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {5 \times 12} {30} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {5 \times 6 \times 2} {5 \times 6} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times \cancel 6 \times 2} {\cancel 5 \times \cancel 6} \\\\
a                                               &= 2 \ \text{inches}
\end{aligned}
$

Number of inches in $5 \ \text{cm} = 2 \ \text{inches}$ 

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{centimeters}                  &:& \text{inches} \\\\
= & 30                                  &:& 12 \\\\
= & 1                                   &:& \dfrac {12} {30} \\\\
= & 1 \times 5                          &:& \dfrac {12 \times 5} {30} \\\\
= & 5                                   &:& \dfrac {6 \times 2 \times 5} {6 \times 5} \\\\
= & 5                                   &:& \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 2 \times \cancel 5} {\cancel 6 \times \cancel 5} \\\\
= & 5 \ \text{cm}                       &:& 2 \ \text{inches}
\end{array}
$

Number of inches in $5 \ \text{cm} = 2 \ \text{inches}$ 

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2 \ \text{inches}$

</div>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The number of centimetres in $9 \ \text{feet} \ 10 \ \text{inches}$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of centimetres $= a$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Measurement in inches}   & = 9 \ \text{feet} \ 10 \ \text{inches} \\\\
                                & = 9 \times 12 \ \text{inches} + 10 \ \text{inches} \\\\
                                &= 108 + 10 \\\\
                                &= 118 \ \text{inches}
\end{aligned}
$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
12 \ \text{inches} : 30 \ \text{cm}             &= 118 \ \text{inches} : a \ \text{cm} \\\\
\dfrac {12} {30}                                &= \dfrac {118} {a} \\\\
12a                                             &= 118 \times 30 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {118 \times 30} {12} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {2 \times 59 \times 6 \times 5} {2 \times 6} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 59 \times \cancel 6 \times 5} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 6} \\\\
a                                               &= 59 \times 5 \\\\
a                                               &= 295 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Number of centimetres in $118 \ \text{inches} = 295 \ \text{cm}$  

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{inches}               &:& \text{centimetres} \\\\
= & 12                          &:& 30 \\\\
= & 1                           &:& \dfrac {30} {12} \\\\
= & 1 \times 118                &:& \dfrac {30 \times 118} {12} \\\\
= & 118                         &:& \dfrac {6 \times 5 \times 2 \times 59} {6 \times 2} \\\\
= & 118                         &:& \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 5 \times \cancel 2 \times 59} {\cancel 6 \times \cancel 2} \\\\
= & 118                         &:& 5 \times 59 \\\\
= & 118 \ \text{inches}         &:& 295 \ \text{cm}
\end{array}
$

Number of centimetres in $118 \ \text{inches} = 295 \ \text{cm}$

</div>
</div>

$295 \ \text {cm}$

</div>

</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>the-haberdashers-askes-boys-school--11-plus--maths--2011--sample-paper-3:1:6</p>

<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>

<div class='rag'>

<div class='rag'>

tratio
</li>

</div>
<div class='question question'>

The drink "Raspberry Heaven" is $1$ part raspberry juice, $2$ parts orange juice and $3$ parts apple juice. 
How much raspberry juice does a $300 \ \text{ml}$ glass of Raspberry Heaven contain?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Using whole to part ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{raspberry heaven}                 &:& \text{raspberry juice} \\\\
= & (1 + 2 + 3) \ \text{ml}                 &:& 1 \ \text{ml} \\\\
= & 6                                       &:& 1 \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {1} {6} \\\\
= & 300                                     &:& \dfrac {300} {6} \\\\
= & 300                                     &:& \dfrac {6 \times 50} {6} \\\\
= & 300                                     &:& \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 50} {\cancel 6} \\\\
= & 300 \ \text{ml}                         &:& 50 \ \text{ml}
\end{array}
$

Volume of Raspberry juice $= 50 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$50 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using whole to part ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{raspberry heaven}                 &:& \text{raspberry juice} \\\\
= & (1 + 2 + 3) \ \text{ml}                 &:& 1 \ \text{ml} \\\\
= & 6                                       &:& 1 \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {1} {6} \\\\
= & 300                                     &:& \dfrac {300} {6} \\\\
= & 300                                     &:& \dfrac {6 \times 50} {6} \\\\
= & 300                                     &:& \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 50} {\cancel 6} \\\\
= & 300 \ \text{ml}                         &:& 50 \ \text{ml}
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Volume of Raspberry juice $= 50 \ \text{ml}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$50 \ \text{ml}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='uuid'>
<p>BDE42C42-21C6-48DE-81AE-A84F76777818</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>the-haberdashers-askes-boys-school--11-plus--maths--2013--sample-paper-4:1:5</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g1</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tratio
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

If a dozen eggs cost $\pounds 2.80$, how much do $18$ eggs cost?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 1 egg}      &= \pounds \dfrac {2.8} {12} \\\\
\text{Cost of 18 egg}     &= 18 \times \dfrac {2.8} {12} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {18 \times 2.8} {12} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {18 \times 28} {12 \times 10} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {6 \times 3 \times 7 \times 4} {3 \times 4 \times 10} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {6 \times \cancel 3 \times 7 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 3 \times \cancel 4 \times 10} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {6 \times 7} {10} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {42} {10} \\\\
                          &= \pounds 4.2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Let the cost of $18$ eggs $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
12 \ \text{eggs} : \pounds 2.8                  &= 18 \ \text{eggs} : \pounds a \\\\
\dfrac {12} {2.8}                                &= \dfrac {18} {a} \\\\
12a                                             &= 18 \times 2.8 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {18 \times 2.8} {12} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {18 \times 28} {12 \times 10} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {6 \times 3 \times 2 \times 14} {6 \times 2 \times 10} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 3 \times \cancel 2 \times 14} {\cancel 6 \times \cancel 2 \times 10} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3 \times 14} {10} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {42} {10} \\\\
a                                               &= \pounds 4.2
\end{aligned}
$

Cost of $18$ eggs $= \pounds 4.2$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{eggs}                 &:& \text{cost} \\\\
= & 12                          &:& \pounds 2.80 \\\\
= & 1                           &:& \dfrac {2.8} {12} \\\\
= & 1 \times 18                 &:& \dfrac {2.8 \times 18} {12} \\\\
= & 18                          &:& \dfrac {28 \times 18} {12 \times 10} \\\\
= & 18                          &:& \dfrac {2 \times 14 \times 3 \times 6} {2 \times 6 \times 10} \\\\
= & 18                          &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 14 \times 3 \times \cancel 6} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 6 \times 10} \\\\
= & 18                          &:& \dfrac {14 \times 3} {10} \\\\
= & 18                          &:& \dfrac {42} {10} \\\\
= & 18                          &:& \pounds 4.2
\end{array}
$

Cost of $18$ eggs $= \pounds 4.2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 4.2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}

                          &= \dfrac {6 \times 7} {10} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {42} {10} \\\\
                          &= \pounds 4.2
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_g1 rag_prns question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>9777149C-D9F9-44DB-9A03-FA447A5348FA</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>the-haberdashers-askes-boys-school--11-plus--maths--2014--sample-paper-5:1:8</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g1</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tratio
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

If $7$ tennis lessons cost $\pounds 167.65$ what is the cost of $1$ lesson?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 1 lesson} &= \dfrac {167.65} {7} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {16765} {7 \times 100} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {2395 \times 7} {7 \times 100} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {2395 \times \cancel {7} } {\cancel {7} \times 100} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {2395} {100} \\\\
                        &= \pounds 23.95
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{lessons}                          &:& \text{cost} \\\\
= & 7                                       &:& \pounds 167.65 \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {167.65} {7} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {16765} {7 \times 100} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {2395 \times 7} {7 \times 100} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {2395 \times \cancel {7} } {\cancel {7} \times 100} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {2395} {100} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \pounds 23.95
\end{array}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 23.95$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 1 lesson} &= \dfrac {167.65} {7} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {16765} {7 \times 100} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {2395 \times 7} {7 \times 100} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {2395 \times \cancel {7} } {\cancel {7} \times 100} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {2395} {100} \\\\
                        &= \pounds 23.95
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{lessons}                          &:& \text{cost} \\\\
= & 7                                       &:& \pounds 167.65 \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {167.65} {7} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {16765} {7 \times 100} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {2395 \times 7} {7 \times 100} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {2395 \times \cancel {7} } {\cancel {7} \times 100} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {2395} {100} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \pounds 23.95
\end{array}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>

</code></pre>

<div class='question question'>

An aircraft takes off from London Heathrow with $120000 \ \text{litres}$ of fuel on board. 
It flies at an average speed of $800 \ \text{kilometres}$ per hour and uses fuel at a rate of $10 \ \text{litres}$ per kilometre.

How much fuel is left in the tank when it lands in Miami $9 \ \text{hours}$ later?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Fuel used}                    &= 800 \times 10 \times 9 \\\\
                                    &= 72000 \ \text{litres} \\\\
\text{Fuel left}                    &= 120000 - 72000 \\\\
                                    &= 48000 \ \text{litres}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$48000 \ \text{litres}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Fuel used}                    &= 800 \times 10 \times 9 \\\\
                                    &= 72000 \ \text{litres} \\\\
\text{Fuel left}                    &= 120000 - 72000 \\\\
                                    &= 48000 \ \text{litres}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$48000 \ \text{litres}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='uuid'>

</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>the-haberdashers-askes-boys-school--11-plus--maths--2017--sample-paper-8:1:20</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>

<div class='rag'>

tratio
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

The sails of a windmill complete one full turn every $40 \ \text{seconds}$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How long does it take the sails to turn through a right angle?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time taken}       &= \dfrac {40} {4} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {10 \times 4} {4} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {10 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 4} \\\\
                        &= 10 \ \text{seconds}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$10 \ \text{seconds}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time taken}       &= \dfrac {40} {4} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {10 \times 4} {4} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {10 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 4} \\\\
                        &= 10 \ \text{seconds}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$10 \ \text{seconds}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many turns do the sails make in fifty-six minutes?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of turns $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
40 \ \text{s} : 1 \ \text{turn}                 &= 56 \times 60 \ \text{s} : a \ \text{turns} \\\\
40                                              &= \dfrac {56 \times 60} {a} \\\\
40 \times a                                     &= 56 \times 60 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {56 \times 60} {40} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {2 \times 28 \times 20 \times 3} {2 \times 20} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 28 \times \cancel {20} \times 3} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel {20}} \\\\
a                                               &= 28 \times 3 \\\\
a                                               &= 84
\end{aligned}
$

Number of turns $= 84$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{seconds}                          &:& \text{turns} \\\\
= & 40 \ \text{s}                           &:& 1 \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {1} {40} \\\\
= & 1 \times 56 \times 60 \ \text{s}        &:& \dfrac {56 \times 60} {40} \\\\
= & 56 \times 60 \ \text{s}                 &:& \dfrac {2 \times 28 \times 20 \times 3} {20 \times 2} \\\\
= & 56 \ \text{minutes}                     &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 28 \times \cancel {20} \times 3} {\cancel {20} \times \cancel 2} \\\\
= & 56 \ \text{minutes}                     &:& 84
\end{array}
$

Number of turns $= 84$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$84$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of turns $= a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
40 \ \text{s} : 1 \ \text{turn}                 &= 56 \times 60 \ \text{s} : a \ \text{turns} \\\\
40                                              &= \dfrac {56 \times 60} {a} \\\\
40 \times a                                     &= 56 \times 60 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {56 \times 60} {40} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {2 \times 28 \times 20 \times 3} {2 \times 20} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 28 \times \cancel {20} \times 3} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel {20}} \\\\
a                                               &= 28 \times 3 \\\\
a                                               &= 84
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of turns $= 84$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{seconds}                          &:& \text{turns} \\\\
= & 40 \ \text{s}                           &:& 1 \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {1} {40} \\\\
= & 1 \times 56 \times 60 \ \text{s}        &:& \dfrac {56 \times 60} {40} \\\\
= & 56 \times 60 \ \text{s}                 &:& \dfrac {2 \times 28 \times 20 \times 3} {20 \times 2} \\\\
= & 56 \ \text{minutes}                     &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 28 \times \cancel {20} \times 3} {\cancel {20} \times \cancel 2} \\\\
= & 56 \ \text{minutes}                     &:& 84
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Number of turns $= 84$
</code></pre>
</div>

![the-independent-schools-examinations-board--11-plus--maths--2009--sample-paper-4/section-1-question-5-00.png](/assets/the-independent-schools-examinations-board--11-plus--maths--2009--sample-paper-4/section-1-question-5-00.png "the-independent-schools-examinations-board--11-plus--maths--2009--sample-paper-4/section-1-question-5-00.png")

<div class='workings'>

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much flour will she need to make $24$ scones?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the flour needed $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
12 \ \text{scones} : 2 \ \text{cups}                      &= 24 \ \text{scones} : a \ \text{cups} \\\\
\dfrac {12} {2}                                           &= \dfrac {24} {a} \\\\
12 \times a                                               &= 24 \times 2 \\\\
a                                                         &= \dfrac {24 \times 2} {12} \\\\
a                                                         &= \dfrac {12 \times 2 \times 2} {12} \\\\
a                                                         &= \dfrac {\cancel {12} \times 2 \times 2} {\cancel {12}} \\\\
a                                                         &= 2 \times 2 \\\\
a                                                         &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

Flour needed $= 4 \ \text{cups}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{scones}                           &:& \text{flour} \\\\
= & 12                                      &:& 2 \ \text{cups} \\\\
= & 12 \times 2                             &:& 2 \times 2 \\\\
= & 24                                      &:& 4 \ \text{cups}
\end{array}
$

Flour needed $= 4 \ \text{cups}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4 \ \text{cups}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the flour needed $= a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
12 \ \text{scones} : 2 \ \text{cups}                      &= 24 \ \text{scones} : a \ \text{cups} \\\\
\dfrac {12} {2}                                           &= \dfrac {24} {a} \\\\
12 \times a                                               &= 24 \times 2 \\\\
a                                                         &= \dfrac {24 \times 2} {12} \\\\
a                                                         &= \dfrac {12 \times 2 \times 2} {12} \\\\
a                                                         &= \dfrac {\cancel {12} \times 2 \times 2} {\cancel {12}} \\\\
a                                                         &= 2 \times 2 \\\\
a                                                         &= 4
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Flour needed $= 4 \ \text{cups}$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{scones}                           &:& \text{flour} \\\\
= & 12                                      &:& 2 \ \text{cups} \\\\
= & 12 \times 2                             &:& 2 \times 2 \\\\
= & 24                                      &:& 4 \ \text{cups}
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Flour needed $= 4 \ \text{cups}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4 \ \text{cups}$
</code></pre>
</div>

</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much salt will she need to make $60$ scones?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the salt needed $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
12 \ \text{scones} : \dfrac {1} {2} \ \text{teaspoons}    &= 60 \ \text{scones} : a \ \text{teaspoons} \\\\
\dfrac {12} {\dfrac {1} {2}}                              &= \dfrac {60} {a} \\\\
12 \times a                                               &= 60 \times \dfrac {1} {2} \\\\
a                                                         &= \dfrac {60} {2 \times 12} \\\\
a                                                         &= \dfrac {12 \times 5} {2 \times 12} \\\\
a                                                         &= \dfrac {\cancel {12} \times 5} {2 \times \cancel {12}} \\\\
a                                                         &= \dfrac {5} {2} \\\\
a                                                         &= 2.5
\end{aligned}
$

Salt needed $= 2.5 \ \text{teaspoons}$

</div>

$

  & \text{scones}                           &:& \text{salt} \\\\

= & 12 \times 5                             &:& \dfrac {5} {2} \\\\
= & 60                                      &:& 2.5 \ \text{teaspoons}
\end{array}
$

Salt needed $= 2.5 \ \text{teaspoons}$

<div class='question subsubquestion'>

Write half a litre in millilitres.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {1} {2} \ \text{litres} \\\\
&= \dfrac {1000} {2} \ \text{ml} \\\\
&= \dfrac {2 \times 500} {2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 500} {\cancel 2} \\\\
&= 500 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$500 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subsubquestion'>
<div class='question subsubquestion'>

What is the maximum number of trays of scones which she can make?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the maximum number of scones made $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
150 \ \text{ml} : 12 \ \text{scones}                      &= 500 \ \text{ml} : a \ \text{scones} \\\\
\dfrac {150} {12}                                         &= \dfrac {500} {a} \\\\
150 \times a                                              &= 500 \times 12 \\\\
a                                                         &= \dfrac {500 \times 12} {150} \\\\
a                                                         &= \dfrac {50 \times 10 \times 3 \times 4} {50 \times 3} \\\\
a                                                         &= \dfrac {\cancel {50} \times 10 \times \cancel 3 \times 4} {\cancel {50} \times \cancel 3} \\\\
a                                                         &= 10 \times 4 \\\\
a                                                         &= 40
\end{aligned}
$

Number of scones made $= 40$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{milk}                             &:& \text{scones} \\\\
= & 150 \ \text{ml}                         &:& 12 \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {12} {150} \\\\
= & 1 \times 500                            &:& \dfrac {12 \times 500} {150} \\\\
= & 500                                     &:& \dfrac {4 \times 3 \times 50 \times 10} {3 \times 50} \\\\
= & 500                                     &:& \dfrac {4 \times \cancel 3 \times \cancel {50} \times 10} {\cancel 3 \times \cancel {50}} \\\\
= & 500                                     &:& 4 \times 10 \\\\
= & 500 \ \text{ml}                         &:& 40
\end{array}
$

Number of scones made $= 40$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$40$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the maximum number of scones made $= a$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

150 \times a                                              &= 500 \times 12 \\\\
a                                                         &= \dfrac {500 \times 12} {150} \\\\
a                                                         &= \dfrac {50 \times 10 \times 3 \times 4} {50 \times 3} \\\\
a                                                         &= \dfrac {\cancel {50} \times 10 \times \cancel 3 \times 4} {\cancel {50} \times \cancel 3} \\\\
a                                                         &= 10 \times 4 \\\\
a                                                         &= 40
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of scones made $= 40$
</code></pre>

\end{array}

<emptyline>

What is the cost of one grapefruit?

Give your answer in pence.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Cost of 1 grapefruit} &= \pounds \dfrac {3.29} {7} \\\\
                             &= \dfrac {329} {7} \ \text {pence} \\\\
                             &= \dfrac {47 \times 7} {7} \\\\
                             &= \dfrac {47 \times \cancel7} {\cancel7} \\\\
                             &= 47 \ \text {pence}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$47 \ \text {pence}$

</div>
</div>

                             &= \dfrac {47 \times 7} {7} \\\\
                             &= \dfrac {47 \times \cancel7} {\cancel7} \\\\
                             &= 47 \ \text {pence}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Change received }    &= \pounds 20 - 47 \times 2 \ \text{pence} \\\\
                            &= \pounds 20 - 94 \ \text{pence} \\\\
                            &= \pounds 20 - \pounds 0.94 \\\\
                            &= \pounds 19.06
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

\end{aligned}

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If he uses $6 \ \text{litres}$ of red paint, how much blue paint should he use? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the blue paint needed $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
2 \ \text{litres} : 3 \ \text{litres}           &= 6 \ \text{litres} : a \ \text{litres} \\\\
\dfrac {2} {3}                                  &= \dfrac {6} {a} \\\\
2 \times a                                      &= 6 \times 3 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {6 \times 3} {2} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {2 \times 3 \times 3} {2} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 3 \times 3} {\cancel 2} \\\\
a                                               &= 3 \times 3 \\\\
a                                               &= 9 \ \text{litres}
\end{aligned}
$

Blue paint needed $= 9 \ \text{litres}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{red paint}                        &:& \text{blue paint} \\\\
= & 2 \ \text{litres}                       &:& 3 \ \text{litres} \\\\
= & 2 \times 3                              &:& 3 \times 3 \\\\
= & 6 \ \text{litres}                       &:& 9 \ \text{litres}
\end{array}
$

Blue paint needed $= 9 \ \text{litres}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9 \ \text {litres}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the blue paint needed $= a$

\end{aligned}
$

</code></pre>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much blue paint is needed to make $35 \ \text{litres}$ of purple paint?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let amount of blue paint used $= a$

Amount of red paint used $= 35 - a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
2 \ \text{litres} : 3 \ \text{litres}           &= (35 - a) \ \text{litres} : a \ \text{litres} \\\\
\dfrac{2} {3}                                   &= \dfrac {35 - a} {a} \\\\
2 \times a                                      &= (35 - a) \times 3 \\\\
2a                                              &= 35 \times 3 - 3a \\\\
2a                                              &= 105 - 3a \\\\
2a + 3a                                         &= 105 \\\\
5a                                              &= 105 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {105} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {5 \times 21} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 21} {\cancel 5} \\\\
a                                               &= 21 \ \text{litres}
\end{aligned}
$

Blue paint needed $= 21 \ \text{litres}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using whole to part ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{purple paint}                     &:& \text{blue paint} \\\\
= & (2 + 3) \ \text{litres}                 &:& 3 \ \text{litres} \\\\
= & 5                                       &:& 3 \\\\
= & 5 \times 7                              &:& 3 \times 7 \\\\
= & 35 \ \text{litres}                      &:& 21 \ \text{litres}
\end{array}
$

Blue paint needed $= 21 \ \text{litres}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$21 \ \text {litres}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let amount of blue paint used $= a$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>
$

a                                               &= \dfrac {105} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {5 \times 21} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 21} {\cancel 5} \\\\
a                                               &= 21 \ \text{litres}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Blue paint needed $= 21 \ \text{litres}$
</code></pre>
</div>

<emptyline>
$

= & 5 \times 7                              &:& 3 \times 7 \\\\
= & 35 \ \text{litres}                      &:& 21 \ \text{litres}
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Blue paint needed $= 21 \ \text{litres}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

<div class='question question'>

A chocolate bar costs $36 \ \text{p}$. How many can Nikita buy for $\pounds 3.00?$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of chocolates}        &= \dfrac {\pounds 3} {36 \ \text{p}} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {300 \ \text{p}} {36 \ \text{p}} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3 \times 4 \times 25} {3 \times 4 \times 3} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times \cancel {4} \times 25} {\cancel {3} \times \cancel {4} \times 3} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {25} {3} \\\\
                                    &= 8 \text{ remainder } 1 \\\\
                                    &\approx 8
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of chocolates}        &= \dfrac {\pounds 3} {36 \ \text{p}} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {300 \ \text{p}} {36 \ \text{p}} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3 \times 4 \times 25} {3 \times 4 \times 3} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times \cancel {4} \times 25} {\cancel {3} \times \cancel {4} \times 3} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {25} {3} \\\\
                                    &= 8 \text{ remainder } 1 \\\\
                                    &\approx 8
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$8$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='uuid'>
<p>80AF6598-5742-4BDF-B71B-8920EC92BDFB</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2008--group-2:1:12</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>

<div class='rag'>

</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Jenny pays $\pounds 156$ for six nights Bed and Breakfast accommodation. How much would it cost her to stay there for $8$ nights?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Amount paid for one night}    &= \pounds \dfrac {156} {6} \\\\
\text{Amount paid for eight nights} &= 8 \times \dfrac {156} {6} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {8 \times 156} {6} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {8 \times 6 \times 26} {6} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {8 \times \cancel 6 \times 26} {\cancel 6} \\\\
                                    &= 8 \times 26 \\\\
                                    &= \pounds 208
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Let the cost to stay for $8$ nights $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
6 \ \text{nights} : \pounds 156                 &= 8 \ \text{nights} : \pounds a \\\\
\dfrac {6} {156}                                &= \dfrac {8} {a} \\\\
6a                                              &= 8 \times 156 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {8 \times 156} {6} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {8 \times 26 \times 6} {6} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {8 \times 26 \times \cancel 6} {\cancel 6} \\\\
a                                               &= 8 \times 26 \\\\
a                                               &= \pounds 208
\end{aligned}
$

Cost to stay for $8$ nights $= \pounds 208$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{nights}               &:& \pounds \\\\
= & 6                           &:& 156 \\\\
= & 1                           &:& \dfrac {156} {6} \\\\
= & 1 \times 8                  &:& \dfrac {156 \times 8} {6} \\\\
= & 8                           &:& \dfrac {6 \times 26 \times 8} {6} \\\\
= & 8                           &:& \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 26 \times 8} {\cancel 6} \\\\
= & 8                           &:& 26 \times 8 \\\\
= & 8                           &:& \pounds 208
\end{array}
$

Cost to stay for $8$ nights $= \pounds 208$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 208$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Amount paid for one night}    &= \pounds \dfrac {156} {6} \\\\
\text{Amount paid for eight nights} &= 8 \times \dfrac {156} {6} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {8 \times 156} {6} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {8 \times 6 \times 26} {6} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {8 \times \cancel 6 \times 26} {\cancel 6} \\\\
                                    &= 8 \times 26 \\\\
                                    &= \pounds 208
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the cost to stay for $8$ nights $= a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
6 \ \text{nights} : \pounds 156                 &= 8 \ \text{nights} : \pounds a \\\\
\dfrac {6} {156}                                &= \dfrac {8} {a} \\\\
6a                                              &= 8 \times 156 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {8 \times 156} {6} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {8 \times 26 \times 6} {6} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {8 \times 26 \times \cancel 6} {\cancel 6} \\\\
a                                               &= 8 \times 26 \\\\
a                                               &= \pounds 208
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Cost to stay for $8$ nights $= \pounds 208$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{nights}               &:& \pounds \\\\
= & 6                           &:& 156 \\\\
= & 1                           &:& \dfrac {156} {6} \\\\
= & 1 \times 8                  &:& \dfrac {156 \times 8} {6} \\\\
= & 8                           &:& \dfrac {6 \times 26 \times 8} {6} \\\\
= & 8                           &:& \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 26 \times 8} {\cancel 6} \\\\
= & 8                           &:& 26 \times 8 \\\\
= & 8                           &:& \pounds 208
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Cost to stay for $8$ nights $= \pounds 208$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

$72$ ladies attending a garden party were each wearing a pair of gloves. 
Some ladies lost their gloves. 
The ratio of ladies who lost gloves to the ladies who did not lose any gloves was $5{:}13$.

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2009--group-1/section-1-question-33-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2009--group-1/section-1-question-33-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2009--group-1/section-1-question-33-00.png")

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What fraction of ladies lost their gloves?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Fraction of ladies lost their gloves}         &= \dfrac {5} {5 + 13} \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {5} {18}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {5} {18}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Fraction of ladies lost their gloves}         &= \dfrac {5} {5 + 13} \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {5} {18}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {5} {18}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$52$ ladies did not lose their gloves.

Some ladies lost one glove and some lost both gloves. 

After the party, the $29$ missing gloves were found.

How many ladies lost only one glove?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Number of ladies who lost their gloves $= 72 - 52 = 20$

Let the ladies who lost only one glove $= a$

Ladies who lost both gloves $= 20 - a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + (20 - a) \times 2                 &= 29 \\\\
a + 40 - 2a                           &= 29 \\\\
40 - 2a + a                           &= 29 \\\\
40 - a                                &= 29 \\\\
40 - 29                               &= a \\\\
a                                     &= 40 - 29 \\\\
a                                     &= 11
\end{aligned}
$

Ladies who lost only one glove $= 11$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$11$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Number of ladies who lost their gloves $= 72 - 52 = 20$
<emptyline>
Let the ladies who lost only one glove $= a$
<emptyline>
Ladies who lost both gloves $= 20 - a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + (20 - a) \times 2                 &= 29 \\\\
a + 40 - 2a                           &= 29 \\\\
40 - 2a + a                           &= 29 \\\\
40 - a                                &= 29 \\\\
40 - 29                               &= a \\\\
a                                     &= 40 - 29 \\\\
a                                     &= 11
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Ladies who lost only one glove $= 11$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$11$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<div class='question subquestion'>

May is $14 \ \text{years}$ old and her father is $39 \ \text{years}$ older than her. 
May's mother is $8 \ \text{years}$ younger than her father. How old is May's mother?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Father's age} &= 14 + 39 \\\\
                    &= 53 \ \text{years} \\\\
\text{Mother's age} &= 53 - 8 \\\\
                    &= 45 \ \text{years}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$45 \ \text{years}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}

                    &= 53 \ \text{years} \\\\
\text{Mother's age} &= 53 - 8 \\\\
                    &= 45 \ \text{years}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Five glass marbles cost a total of $\pounds 7.50$. How many marbles could I buy with $\pounds 24?$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of marbles in 1 pound}   &= \dfrac {5} {7.5} \\\\
\text{Number of marbles in 24 pounds} &= 24 \times \dfrac {5} {7.5} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {24 \times 5} {7.5} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {24 \times 5 \times 10} {75} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {3 \times 8 \times 5 \times 5 \times 2} {5 \times 5 \times 3} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 8 \times \cancel 5 \times \cancel 5 \times 2} {\cancel 5 \times \cancel 5 \times \cancel 3} \\\\
                                      &= 8 \times 2 \\\\
                                      &= 16
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of marbles $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
\pounds 7.5 : 5 \ \text{marbles}                &= \pounds 24 : a \ \text{marbles} \\\\
\dfrac {7.5} {5}                                &= \dfrac {24} {a} \\\\
7.5 \times a                                    &= 24 \times 5 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {24 \times 5} {7.5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {24 \times 5 \times 10} {75} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {120 \times 50 \times 2} {50} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3 \times 8 \times 5 \times 5 \times 2} {5 \times 5 \times 3} \\\\
a                                               &= 8 \times 2 \\\\
a                                               &= 16
\end{aligned}
$

Number of marbles $= 16$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{cost}                             &:& \text{marbles} \\\\
= & \pounds 7.5                             &:& 5 \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {5} {7.5} \\\\
= & 1 \times 24                             &:& \dfrac {24 \times 5} {7.5} \\\\
= & 24                                      &:& \dfrac {24 \times 5 \times 10} {75} \\\\
= & 24                                      &:& \dfrac {8 \times 3 \times 5 \times 5 \times 2} {3 \times 5 \times 5} \\\\
= & 24                                      &:& \dfrac {8 \times \cancel 3 \times \cancel 5 \times \cancel 5 \times 2} {\cancel 3 \times \cancel 5 \times \cancel 5} \\\\
= & 24                                      &:& 8 \times 2 \\\\
= & \pounds 24                              &:& 16
\end{array}
$

Number of marbles $= 16$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$16$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of marbles in 1 pound}   &= \dfrac {5} {7.5} \\\\
\text{Number of marbles in 24 pounds} &= 24 \times \dfrac {5} {7.5} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {24 \times 5} {7.5} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {24 \times 5 \times 10} {75} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {3 \times 8 \times 5 \times 5 \times 2} {5 \times 5 \times 3} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 8 \times \cancel 5 \times \cancel 5 \times 2} {\cancel 5 \times \cancel 5 \times \cancel 3} \\\\
                                      &= 8 \times 2 \\\\
                                      &= 16
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of marbles $= a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

\dfrac {7.5} {5}                                &= \dfrac {24} {a} \\\\
7.5 \times a                                    &= 24 \times 5 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {24 \times 5} {7.5} \\\\

a                                               &= \dfrac {120 \times 50 \times 2} {50} \\\\

\end{aligned}
$

Number of marbles $= 16$
</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2009--group-2:1:14</p>

<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>

<div class='rag'>

<div class='question question'>

Disc $B$ turns twice when $A$ turns $5$ times. If disc $A$ turns $45$ times, how many times does disc $B$ turn?

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2009--group-2/section-1-question-14-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2009--group-2/section-1-question-14-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2009--group-2/section-1-question-14-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of times disc B turned $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
5 \ \text{disc A} : 2 \ \text{disc B}           &= 45 \ \text{disc A} : a \ \text{disc B} \\\\
\dfrac{5} {2}                                   &= \dfrac {45} {a} \\\\
5a                                              &= 45 \times 2 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {45 \times 2} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times 5 \times 2} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times \cancel 5 \times 2} {\cancel 5} \\\\
a                                               &= 9 \times 2 \\\\
a                                               &= 18
\end{aligned}
$

Number of times disc B turned $= 18$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{disc A}                         &:& \text{disc B} \\\\
= & 5                                     &:& 2 \\\\
= & 1                                     &:& \dfrac{2} {5} \\\\
= & 1 \times 45                           &:& \dfrac{2 \times 45} {5} \\\\
= & 45                                    &:& \dfrac{2 \times 5 \times 9} {5} \\\\
= & 45                                    &:& \dfrac{2 \times \cancel 5 \times 9} {\cancel 5} \\\\
= & 45                                    &:& 2 \times 9 \\\\
= & 45                                    &:& 18
\end{array}
$

Number of times disc B turned $= 18$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$18$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of times disc B turned $= a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
5 \ \text{disc A} : 2 \ \text{disc B}           &= 45 \ \text{disc A} : a \ \text{disc B} \\\\
\dfrac{5} {2}                                   &= \dfrac {45} {a} \\\\
5a                                              &= 45 \times 2 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {45 \times 2} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times 5 \times 2} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times \cancel 5 \times 2} {\cancel 5} \\\\
a                                               &= 9 \times 2 \\\\
a                                               &= 18
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of times disc B turned $= 18$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{disc A}                         &:& \text{disc B} \\\\
= & 5                                     &:& 2 \\\\
= & 1                                     &:& \dfrac{2} {5} \\\\
= & 1 \times 45                           &:& \dfrac{2 \times 45} {5} \\\\
= & 45                                    &:& \dfrac{2 \times 5 \times 9} {5} \\\\
= & 45                                    &:& \dfrac{2 \times \cancel 5 \times 9} {\cancel 5} \\\\
= & 45                                    &:& 2 \times 9 \\\\
= & 45                                    &:& 18
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Number of times disc B turned $= 18$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$18$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='uuid'>
<p>FC90867D-22A0-411A-B1A0-45BFD51CF3A0</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2010--group-1:1:14</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g1</p>

<p>rag_wf_prns</p>

<li>
tratio

<div class='question question'>

Justin wants to make some bird tables to raise money for a charity. 
To make $4$ bird tables he needs:

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2010--group-1/section-1-question-14-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2010--group-1/section-1-question-14-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2010--group-1/section-1-question-14-00.png")

$16 \ \text{metres}$ of wood

$4$ wooden trays

$64$ nails.

Write out the list of materials he would need to make $10$ bird tables.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{bird tables}  &:& \text{wooden trays}                           &:& \text{wood}                                           &:& \text{nails} \\\\
= & 4                   &:& 4                                             &:& 16 \ \text{m}                                         &:& 64 \\\\
= & 1                   &:& \dfrac {4} {4}                                &:& \dfrac {16} {4}                                       &:& \dfrac {64} {4} \\\\
= & 1 \times 10         &:& \dfrac {4 \times 10} {4}                      &:& \dfrac {16 \times 10} {4}                             &:& \dfrac {64 \times 10} {4} \\\\
= & 10                  &:& \dfrac {4 \times 10} {4}                      &:& \dfrac {4 \times 4 \times 10} {4}                     &:& \dfrac {4 \times 16 \times 10} {4} \\\\
= & 10                  &:& \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 10} {\cancel 4}      &:& \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 4 \times 10} {\cancel 4}     &:& \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 16 \times 10} {\cancel 4} \\\\
= & 10                  &:& 10                                            &:& 4 \times 10                                           &:& 16 \times 10 \\\\
= & 10                  &:& 10                                            &:& 40 \ \text{m}                                         &:& 160 \\\\
\end{array}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

wooden trays $=$ $10$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

wood $=$ $40 \ \text{m}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

nails $=$ $160$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{bird tables}  &:& \text{wooden trays}                           &:& \text{wood}                                           &:& \text{nails} \\\\
= & 4                   &:& 4                                             &:& 16 \ \text{m}                                         &:& 64 \\\\
= & 1                   &:& \dfrac {4} {4}                                &:& \dfrac {16} {4}                                       &:& \dfrac {64} {4} \\\\
= & 1 \times 10         &:& \dfrac {4 \times 10} {4}                      &:& \dfrac {16 \times 10} {4}                             &:& \dfrac {64 \times 10} {4} \\\\
= & 10                  &:& \dfrac {4 \times 10} {4}                      &:& \dfrac {4 \times 4 \times 10} {4}                     &:& \dfrac {4 \times 16 \times 10} {4} \\\\
= & 10                  &:& \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 10} {\cancel 4}      &:& \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 4 \times 10} {\cancel 4}     &:& \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 16 \times 10} {\cancel 4} \\\\
= & 10                  &:& 10                                            &:& 4 \times 10                                           &:& 16 \times 10 \\\\
= & 10                  &:& 10                                            &:& 40 \ \text{m}                                         &:& 160 \\\\
\end{array}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>wooden trays</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$10$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>wood</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$40 \ \text{m}$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>nails</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$160$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_g1 rag_prns question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>36A2225F-49FB-4E71-9561-C6253B697DCB</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2011--group-2:1:34</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>

<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g1</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>

<div class='topics'>

tratio

</div>
<div class='question question'>

A factory recycles paper cups for use in its canteen. Seven used cups 
are needed to make each new cup. From $721$ used cups, how many new cups 
can be made in total, if all the used cups get recycled?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total new cups} &= \dfrac {721} {7} \\\\
                      &= \dfrac {103 \times 7} {7} \\\\
                      &= \dfrac {103 \times \cancel {7}} {\cancel {7}} \\\\
                      &= 103
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$103$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total new cups} &= \dfrac {721} {7} \\\\
                      &= \dfrac {103 \times 7} {7} \\\\
                      &= \dfrac {103 \times \cancel {7}} {\cancel {7}} \\\\
                      &= 103
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$103$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>


<div class='review'>

</div>
<div class='workings'>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many stickers can be bought for $\pounds 2.40?$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of stickers for 1p}            &= \dfrac {3} {40} \\\\
\text{Number of stickers for \pounds 2.40}  &= \dfrac {3} {40} \times 2.40 \times 100 \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {3 \times 240} {40} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {3 \times 40 \times 6} {40} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {40} \times 6} {\cancel {40}} \\\\
                                            &= 3 \times 6 \\\\
                                            &= 18
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of stickers $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
40 \ \text{p} : 3 \ \text{stickers}               &= 2.40 \times 100 \ \text{p} : a \ \text{stickers} \\\\
\dfrac{40} {3}                                  &= \dfrac {2.40 \times 100} {a} \\\\
40 \times a                                     &= 2.40 \times 100 \times 3 \\\\
40 \times a                                     &= 240 \times 3 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {240 \times 3} {40} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {40 \times 6 \times 3} {40} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {40} \times 6 \times 3} {\cancel {40}} \\\\
a                                               &= 6 \times 3 \\\\
a                                               &= 18
\end{aligned}
$

Number of stickers $= 18$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{cost}                             &:& \text{stickers} \\\\
= & 40 \ \text{p}                             &:& 3 \\\\
= & 40 \times 6                             &:& 3 \times 6 \\\\
= & 240 \ \text{p}                            &:& 18 \\\\
= & \pounds 2.40                            &:& 18
\end{array}
$

Number of stickers $= 18$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$18$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}

                                            &= \dfrac {3 \times 240} {40} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {3 \times 40 \times 6} {40} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {40} \times 6} {\cancel {40}} \\\\

\end{aligned}
$

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much  would $33$ stickers cost? *(Give your answer in pounds.)*

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 1 sticker}      &= \dfrac {40} {3} \ \text{p} \\\\
\text{Cost of 33 stickers}    &= \dfrac {40} {3} \times 33 \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {40 \times 33} {3} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {40 \times 3 \times 11} {3} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {40 \times \cancel 3 \times 11} {\cancel 3} \\\\
                              &= 40 \times 11 \\\\
                              &= 440 \ \text{p} \\\\
                              &= \pounds 4.40
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Let the cost of stickers $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
3 \ \text{stickers} : 40 \ \text{p}               &= 33 \ \text{stickers} : a \ \text{p} \\\\
\dfrac{3} {40}                                  &= \dfrac {33} {a} \\\\
3 \times a                                      &= 33 \times 40 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {33 \times 40} {3} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3 \times 11 \times 40} {3} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 11 \times 40} {\cancel 3} \\\\
a                                               &= 11 \times 40 \\\\
a                                               &= 440 \ \text{p} \\\\
a                                               &= \pounds 4.40
\end{aligned}
$

Cost of stickers $= \pounds 4.4$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{stickers}                         &:& \text{cost} \\\\
= & 3                                       &:& 40 \ \text{p} \\\\
= & 3 \times 11                             &:& 40 \times 11 \ \text{p} \\\\
= & 3 \times 11                             &:& 440 \ \text{p} \\\\
= & 33                                      &:& \pounds 4.40
\end{array}
$

Cost of stickers $= \pounds 4.4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 4.40$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 1 sticker}      &= \dfrac {40} {3} \ \text{p} \\\\
\text{Cost of 33 stickers}    &= \dfrac {40} {3} \times 33 \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {40 \times 33} {3} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {40 \times 3 \times 11} {3} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {40 \times \cancel 3 \times 11} {\cancel 3} \\\\
                              &= 40 \times 11 \\\\
                              &= 440 \ \text{p} \\\\
                              &= \pounds 4.40
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the cost of stickers $= a$

<emptyline>

a                                               &= \dfrac {3 \times 11 \times 40} {3} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 11 \times 40} {\cancel 3} \\\\
a                                               &= 11 \times 40 \\\\
a                                               &= 440 \ \text{p} \\\\
a                                               &= \pounds 4.40
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Cost of stickers $= \pounds 4.4$
</code></pre>

\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
