</code></pre>

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 1 packet}          &= \pounds \dfrac {3.43} {7} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {343} {7} \ \text {pence} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {49 \times 7} {7} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {49 \times \cancel {7}} {\cancel {7}} \\\\
                                 &= 49 \ \text {pence}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

<div class='answer'>

</div>
</div>

                                 &= \dfrac {49 \times \cancel {7}} {\cancel {7}} \\\\
                                 &= 49 \ \text {pence}
\end{aligned}
$

\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 2 packets}      &= 3.43 \times 2 \\\\
                              &= \pounds 6.86 \\\\

                              &= \pounds 13.14
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many bales of hay does Jasper eat in $8\ \text{days}?$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of bales of hay}     &= 8 \times \dfrac {3} {4} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {8 \times 3} {4} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {4 \times 2 \times 3} {4} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 2 \times 3} {\cancel 4} \\\\
                                  &= 2 \times 3 \\\\
                                  &= 6
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of bales of hay $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
1 \ \text{days} : \dfrac {3} {4} \ \text{bales} &= 8 \ \text{days} : a \ \text{bales} \\\\
\dfrac{1} {\dfrac {3} {4}}                      &= \dfrac {8} {a} \\\\
a                                               &= 8 \times \dfrac {3} {4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {8 \times 3} {4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {4 \times 2 \times 3} {4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 2 \times 3} {\cancel 4} \\\\
a                                               &= 2 \times 3 \\\\
a                                               &= 6
\end{aligned}
$

Number of bales of hay $= 6$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{days}                             &:& \text{bales} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {3} {4} \\\\
= & 1 \times 8                              &:& \dfrac {3 \times 8} {4} \\\\
= & 8                                       &:& \dfrac {3 \times 4 \times 2} {4} \\\\
= & 8                                       &:& \dfrac {8 \times \cancel 4 \times 2} {\cancel 4} \\\\
= & 8                                       &:& 8 \times 2 \\\\
= & 8                                       &:& 16
\end{array}
$

Number of bales of hay $= 6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$16$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$

                                  &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 2 \times 3} {\cancel 4} \\\\

\end{aligned}
$

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many days does it take Jasper to eat $27 \ \text{bales}$ of hay?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time taken for 1 bale}      &= \dfrac {1} {\dfrac {3} {4}} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {4} {3} \ \text{days} \\\\
\text{Time taken for 27 bales}    &= 27 \times \dfrac {4} {3} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {27 \times 4} {3} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {3 \times 9 \times 4} {3} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 9 \times 4} {\cancel 3} \\\\
                                  &= 9 \times 4 \\\\
                                  &= 36 \ \text{days}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of days $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {3} {4} \ \text{bales} : 1 \ \text{days} &= 27 \ \text{bales} : a \ \text{days} \\\\
\dfrac {3} {4}                                  &= \dfrac {27} {a} \\\\
3 \times a                                      &= 27 \times 4 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {27 \times 4} {3} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3 \times 9 \times 4} {3} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 9 \times 4} {\cancel 3} \\\\
a                                               &= 9 \times 4 \\\\
a                                               &= 36
\end{aligned}
$

Number of days $= 36$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{bales}                            &:& \text{days} \\\\
= & \dfrac {3} {4}                          &:& 1 \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {4} {3} \\\\
= & 1 \times 27                             &:& \dfrac {4 \times 27} {3} \\\\
= & 27                                      &:& \dfrac {4 \times \cancel 3 \times 9} {\cancel 3} \\\\
= & 27                                      &:& 4 \times 9 \\\\
= & 27                                      &:& 36
\end{array}
$

Number of days $= 36$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$36$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time taken for 1 bale}      &= \dfrac {1} {\dfrac {3} {4}} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {4} {3} \ \text{days} \\\\
\text{Time taken for 27 bales}    &= 27 \times \dfrac {4} {3} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {27 \times 4} {3} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {3 \times 9 \times 4} {3} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 9 \times 4} {\cancel 3} \\\\
                                  &= 9 \times 4 \\\\
                                  &= 36 \ \text{days}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of days $= a$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>

\end{aligned}

\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Number of days $= 36$

<div class='uuid'>
<p>2A0FA070-25B8-4F23-B272-463207966E46</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2014--group-2:1:21</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>

<div class='rag'>

tratio
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Mr Cooper's car uses $3 \ \text{litres}$ of petrol to travel $50 \ \text{kilometres}$.

Petrol costs $\pounds 1.45$ per litre.

How much will Mr Cooper spend on petrol to drive $600 \ \text{kilometres}$ from Oxford to Glasgow?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Petrol needed for 1 km}      &= \dfrac {3} {50} \\\\
\text {Petrol needed for 600 km}    &= \dfrac {3} {50} \times 600 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3 \times 600} {50} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3 \times 50 \times 12} {50} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {50} \times 12} {\cancel {50}} \\\\
                                    &= 3 \times 12 \\\\
                                    &= 36 \ \text{litres} \\\\
\text {Cost of the petrol}          &= 1.45 \times 36 \\\\
                                    &= \pounds 52.2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{km}                                     &:& \text{litres} \\\\
= & 50                                            &:& 3 \\\\
= & 1                                             &:& \dfrac {3} {50} \\\\
= & 1 \times 600                                  &:& \dfrac {3 \times 600} {50} \\\\
= & 600                                           &:& \dfrac {3 \times 50 \times 12} {50} \\\\
= & 600                                           &:& \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {50} \times 12} {\cancel {50}} \\\\
= & 600                                           &:& 3 \times 12 \\\\
= & 600 \ \text{km}                               &:& 36 \ \text{litres}
\end{array}
$

<br>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Petrol needed}       &= 36 \ \text{litres} \\\\
\text {Cost of the petrol}  &= 1.45 \times 36 \\\\
                            &= \pounds 52.2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 52.2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Petrol needed for 1 km}      &= \dfrac {3} {50} \\\\
\text {Petrol needed for 600 km}    &= \dfrac {3} {50} \times 600 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3 \times 600} {50} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3 \times 50 \times 12} {50} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {50} \times 12} {\cancel {50}} \\\\
                                    &= 3 \times 12 \\\\
                                    &= 36 \ \text{litres} \\\\

                                    &= \pounds 52.2
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

Below are the instructions for *Kleeno*, a new kitchen disinfectant.

Instructions: Mix $20\ \text{ml}$ of Kleeno with $4\ \text{litres}$ of water.

A bottle of Kleeno contains $540 \ \text{ml}$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many $20 \ \text{ml}$ portions of *Kleeno* are contained in one bottle?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of portions}             &= \dfrac {540} {20} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {20 \times 27} {20} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {\cancel {20} \times 27} {\cancel {20}} \\\\
                                      &= 27
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$27$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of portions}             &= \dfrac {540} {20} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {20 \times 27} {20} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {\cancel {20} \times 27} {\cancel {20}} \\\\
                                      &= 27
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$27$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much *Kleeno* needs to be added to a bucket containing $10 \ \text{litres}$ of water?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of Kleeno needed $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
4 \ \text{litres} : 20 \ \text{ml}              &= 10 \ \text{litres} : a \ \text{ml} \\\\
\dfrac{4} {20}                                  &= \dfrac {10} {a} \\\\
4 \times a                                      &= 10 \times 20 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {10 \times 20} {4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {10 \times 4 \times 5} {4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {10 \times \cancel 4 \times 5} {\cancel 4} \\\\
a                                               &= 10 \times 5 \\\\
a                                               &= 50 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$

Kleeno needed $= 50 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{water}                            &:& \text{kleeno} \\\\
= & 4 \ \text{litres}                       &:& 20 \ \text{ml} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {20} {4} \\\\
= & 1 \times 10                             &:& \dfrac {20 \times 10} {4} \\\\
= & 10                                      &:& \dfrac {4 \times 5 \times 10} {4} \\\\
= & 10                                      &:& \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 5 \times 10} {\cancel 4} \\\\
= & 10                                      &:& 5 \times 10 \\\\
= & 10 \ \text{litres}                      &:& 50 \ \text{ml}
\end{array}
$

Kleeno needed $= 50 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$50 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

a                                               &= \dfrac {10 \times \cancel 4 \times 5} {\cancel 4} \\\\
a                                               &= 10 \times 5 \\\\
a                                               &= 50 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Kleeno needed $= 50 \ \text{ml}$
</code></pre>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Maria uses $6 \ \text{litres}$ of water every time she cleans her kitchen.

She cleans her kitchen every day except for Sunday.

For how many weeks does a bottle of *Kleeno* last her?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of Kleeno needed $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
4 \ \text{litres} : 20 \ \text{ml}              &= 6 \ \text{litres} : a \ \text{ml} \\\\
\dfrac{4} {20}                                  &= \dfrac {6} {a} \\\\
4 \times a                                      &= 6 \times 20 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {6 \times 20} {4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {6 \times 4 \times 5} {4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {6 \times \cancel 4 \times 5} {\cancel 4} \\\\
a                                               &= 6 \times 5 \\\\
a                                               &= 30 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of weeks}      &= \dfrac {540} {30 \times 6} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {30 \times 6 \times 3} {30 \times 6} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {\cancel {30} \times \cancel 6 \times 3} {\cancel {30} \times \cancel 6} \\\\
                            &= 3
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{water}                            &:& \text{kleeno} \\\\
= & 4 \ \text{litres}                       &:& 20 \ \text{ml} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {20} {4} \\\\
= & 1 \times 6                              &:& \dfrac {20 \times 6} {4} \\\\
= & 6                                       &:& \dfrac {4 \times 5 \times 6} {4} \\\\
= & 6                                       &:& \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 5 \times 6} {\cancel 4} \\\\
= & 6                                       &:& 5 \times 6 \\\\
= & 6 \ \text{litres}                       &:& 30 \ \text{ml}
\end{array}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of weeks}      &= \dfrac {540} {30 \times 6} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {30 \times 6 \times 3} {30 \times 6} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {\cancel {30} \times \cancel 6 \times 3} {\cancel {30} \times \cancel 6} \\\\
                            &= 3
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the amount of Kleeno needed $= a$

$
\begin{aligned}

a                                               &= \dfrac {6 \times 20} {4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {6 \times 4 \times 5} {4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {6 \times \cancel 4 \times 5} {\cancel 4} \\\\

a                                               &= 30 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of weeks}      &= \dfrac {540} {30 \times 6} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {30 \times 6 \times 3} {30 \times 6} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {\cancel {30} \times \cancel 6 \times 3} {\cancel {30} \times \cancel 6} \\\\
                            &= 3
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>

                            &= \dfrac {30 \times 6 \times 3} {30 \times 6} \\\\

                            &= 3

$

<div class='question question'>

A tea leaf weighs $0.008 \ \text{grams}$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What would be the mass of $1000$ tea leaves?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Mass of 1000 tea leaves}  &= \text {Mass of one tea leaf} \times 1000 \\\\
                                 &= 0.008 \times 1000 \\\\
                                 &= 8 \ \text {grams}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8 \ \text {grams}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Mass of 1000 tea leaves}  &= \text {Mass of one tea leaf} \times 1000 \\\\
                                 &= 0.008 \times 1000 \\\\
                                 &= 8 \ \text {grams}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$8 \ \text {grams}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many tea leaves would you expect there to be in a $1 \ \text{kg}$ pack of tea?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of tea leaves}      &= \dfrac {1000} {0.008} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {1000 \times 1000} {8} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {1000 \times 125 \times 8} {8} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {1000 \times 125 \times \cancel 8} {\cancel 8} \\\\
                                  &= 1000 \times 125 \\\\
                                  &= 125000
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$125000$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of tea leaves}      &= \dfrac {1000} {0.008} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {1000 \times 1000} {8} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {1000 \times 125 \times 8} {8} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {1000 \times 125 \times \cancel 8} {\cancel 8} \\\\
                                  &= 1000 \times 125 \\\\
                                  &= 125000
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$125000$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

A tea bag contains $250$ tea leaves.

The tissue covering weighs $0.5 \ \text{g}$.

How many tea bags will there be in a $250 \text{g}$ packet of tea bags?

(The $250 \text{g}$ includes the mass of the bags and not just the tea leaves.)

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of tea bags}        &= \dfrac {\text {Total mass}} {\text{Mass of 1 tea bag}} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {250} {250 \times 0.008 + 0.5} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {250} {2 + 0.5} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {250} {2.5} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {250 \times 10} {25} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {25 \times 10 \times 10} {25} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {\cancel {25} \times 10 \times 10} {\cancel {25}} \\\\
                                  &= 100
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$100 \ \text {bags}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of tea bags}        &= \dfrac {\text {Total mass}} {\text{Mass of 1 tea bag}} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {250} {250 \times 0.008 + 0.5} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {250} {2 + 0.5} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {250} {2.5} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {250 \times 10} {25} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {25 \times 10 \times 10} {25} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {\cancel {25} \times 10 \times 10} {\cancel {25}} \\\\
                                  &= 100
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$100 \ \text {bags}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
